## Hunger & Thirst survival meters

Your character has two survival needs that tick down the whole time you're in the world: **Food (hunger)** and **H2O (thirst)**. Let them hit empty and you get stuck at a walk — no sprinting, no running away, no keeping up with a chase. Keep them topped off and you actually heal minor wounds for free. Both start at **100** and count down to **0**.

### Where you see it (the HUD)

Bottom-right of your screen there's a stack of segmented bars:

- **HP** (top)
- **H2O / water** (middle)
- **FOOD / hunger** (bottom)
- **WANTED** bar appears under them if you're a wanted civ

The water and food bars are **cyan** when you're fine, turn **amber below 40**, and go **red below 20**. That colour flip is your at-a-glance "go eat/drink now" signal. There's no separate menu — the bars are always on-screen while you're alive.

### How the meters drain

Nothing you do speeds it up much; it's mostly just time passing:

- **Thirst:** loses **10 points about every 12.5 minutes**.
- **Hunger:** loses **10 points about every 15 minutes**.
- **Walking penalty:** on top of that, roughly **every ~20 minutes of continuous travel on foot** you lose an **extra 5 hunger AND 5 thirst** at once. (verify exact real-time — it counts actual movement, not idle time.) Riding in a vehicle does **not** rack up this walking drain.

So from a full 100, thirst empties in roughly **2 hours** and hunger in roughly **2.5 hours** if you just stand around — faster if you're out running the map.

**Good news:** the meters are **paused** while you're sitting on the spawn-select screen or while you're **restrained/handcuffed**, so you won't starve to death AFK at spawn or while a cop is dragging you around.

### Warnings and the starving/dehydrated penalty

You get on-screen text warnings as each meter drops past certain marks:

**Hunger:**
- **30:** "You haven't eaten anything in awhile. You should find something to eat soon!"
- **20:** "You are starting to starve, you need to find something to eat."
- **10:** "You are now starving, you must eat something."

**Thirst:**
- **30:** "You haven't had anything to drink in a while..."
- **20:** "You haven't had anything to drink in a long time..."
- **10:** "You are now suffering from severe dehydration, find something to drink quickly!"

**The real penalty happens at 0.** If **either** meter hits **empty (0)**, you're **forced to walk** — you physically cannot sprint or run until you eat/drink back above 0. (This is the same forced-walk you get from being over-weight or hauling a gold bar.) The old "fatigue" messages you'll still see (e.g. after over-eating) are mostly cosmetic now — sprint stamina was removed server-wide, so the only movement hit that matters is the forced-walk at 0.

Exception: the forced-walk is **skipped while you're arrested**, and it's **lifted the moment you're in/over water** so you don't sink and drown while starved (you can still swim).

### Eating — food and exact hunger values

Select a food item in your inventory and hit **Use**. Each food restores hunger by a set amount:

| Food | Hunger restored |
|---|---|
| Ginger | +5 |
| Wild Berry | +5 hunger **and +5 thirst** |
| Apple | +10 |
| Peach | +10 |
| Cooked Rabbit / Cooked Chicken | +20 |
| Ornate (fish) | +25 |
| Cooked Dog / Salema / Mackerel / Donuts | +30 |
| Banana / Tinned Bacon / Red Burger | +40 |
| Kebab | +60 |
| Mullet (fish) | +80 |
| Doner Kebab | ~+86 |
| Tuna / Catshark / Turtle / Turtle Soup / Cooked Human Flesh | +100 |

- **Red Burger** normally gives **+40**, but if you're **high/smoking** it satisfies the munchies for **+80** instead.
- **Fish** (salema, ornate, mackerel, tuna, mullet, catshark, turtle) are eaten **directly** — no cooking needed.
- **Over-eating:** if a food would push hunger past 100 it just caps at 100 and you get an "over-eaten, feeling fatigued" message (harmless flavor now).

### Drinking — what actually refills thirst

Select the drink and hit **Use**:

- **Water Bottle**, **Soda**, and **Coffee** each refill thirst **all the way to 100** in one go.
- **Red Gull** also refills thirst **to 100** — plus a **+10% sprint-speed buff** for a few minutes.
- **Wild Berry** (eaten) adds **+5 thirst** alongside its +5 hunger.
- **Beer** adds **+10 thirst**, and **moonshine / scotch / rum** add **+10 thirst** each — but they raise your alcohol level and blur/distort your screen, so they're a bad way to hydrate.

### Cooking raw meat

Hunting animals (rabbit, chicken, dog, sheep, goat, snake) and gutting human corpses gives you **raw** meat, which you **can't eat raw** — you have to cook it first.

1. Get a fire going: buy a **Campfire Kit** (~$500) and light it, or use any **burning metal barrel**.
2. Walk up to the lit fire, open the **scroll-wheel menu**, and choose **"Cook Raw Meat"** (you must be within ~4m and the fire must actually be burning).
3. All your raw meat gets converted to its cooked version (e.g. raw rabbit → Cooked Rabbit) and is then edible for the values in the table above. Cooking each stack takes a couple of seconds.

**Cooking human flesh is a bad idea:** cooked Human Flesh restores +100 hunger, but there's a **~10% chance** it makes you violently ill — it **zeroes both your hunger and thirst** at once and lands you a **wanted charge** (verify which). It also sends out a public "you smell burning flesh" broadcast with your bearing/distance to nearby players and cops, so it's not subtle.

### Collecting water (read this — it does NOT quench thirst)

There's a **"Collect Water"** action (scroll-wheel, or the action key while standing in shallow water on foot, water depth under ~1m). It gathers **2 Salt Water every ~5 seconds** until your inventory is full.

**Important gotcha:** Salt Water is **not drinkable**. You cannot use it to refill your thirst bar — it's purely a **distilling ingredient** for making spirits (rum, scotch, whiskey). New players constantly wade into the sea expecting to drink; it does nothing for thirst. To actually hydrate you must **buy Water Bottles / Soda / Coffee / Red Gull** (or grab Wild Berries for a small +5).

### Passive health regen — the reward for staying topped up

If you keep **both** meters at **75 or higher**, your character **slowly heals minor wounds automatically** — about **1% HP every 5 seconds**. Conditions: you have to be **injured but not badly** (roughly 75%–100% HP), your legs can't be badly hurt, you can't be downed, and you can't be in the post-revive window. Practically: eat and drink to keep both bars in the top quarter and small scrapes patch themselves up without a medic or a First Aid Kit. Let a meter drop below 75 and this healing stops.

### Talents that slow the drain

You can spend talent points to make food and drink last much longer. This only slows the **timed** drain, not the walking penalty:

- **"Hunger Games"** (civilian) — **consumption reduced ~75%** (you drain ~3 per tick instead of 10). Huge for long fishing/gathering runs.
- **"Nourished"** (police) — **consumption reduced ~25%**.
- **"Hunger Games"** (police, upgrade of Nourished) — **consumption reduced ~50%**.

Check the **Talents** app on your phone to assign these.

### Where to buy food & drink (and rough prices)

Just about every shop stocks basic food and water. Prices below are the approximate market price (verify exact per-shop numbers):

- **Water Bottle** ~$7 · **Soda** ~$7 · **Coffee** ~$6
- **Apple** ~$6 · **Peach** ~$6 · **Wild Berry** ~$12 · **Donuts** ~$13
- **Kebab** ~$100 · **Doner Kebab** ~$85 · **Red Gull** ~$160
- **Campfire Kit** ~$500 (for cooking meat)

Handy stops:
- **Gas Station Market** — water, apple, Red Gull, kebab, doner kebab, tinned bacon, campfire.
- **Red Burger** — red burgers, soda, water, coffee, beer.
- **General/market shops** — water, apple, peach, beer, Red Gull, kebabs, berries, plus raw/cooked meats.
- **Rebel & Turf Markets** — water, apple, Red Gull, tinned bacon.
- **Police shop** — donuts, coffee, water, apple, Red Gull (for cops on duty).

### Gotchas / tips

- **Salt Water ≠ drinking water.** Wading in the sea will never refill your thirst bar. Buy bottled water/soda/coffee.
- **Being at 0 = you're stuck walking.** In a firefight or a chase, an empty meter is a death sentence. Carry a couple of water bottles and a snack.
- **Warnings can be skipped.** Because the walking penalty knocks off 5 at a time, your meter can jump past the 30/20/10 warning marks without ever showing that exact message — watch the **bar colour** (amber/red), don't rely on the text popups.
- **Death, respawn, jail all reset both meters to 100.** Dying is a full "refill," so there's no need to eat right before a risky fight you expect to lose.
- **Meters persist across relogs** — if you log back into your saved world spot, you resume at the hunger/thirst you left with (not a free reset). Log out starving, log in starving.
- **Keep both ≥75 to auto-heal.** Cheapest "medkit" in the game is a water bottle and an apple.
- **Fish don't need cooking; land-animal and snake meat do.** Don't waste a campfire trip on tuna.

---

## Groups / Party system (temporary squads, NOT gangs)

A **Group** (also called a party or squad) is a quick, throwaway team you and some friends can form on the spot. It is **not** a gang: nothing here is saved to your character. Groups live only in memory, so they vanish on a server restart and there is no roster to log back into. Use a group when you want to run together for an afternoon; use a **gang** (separate system, "My Gang" on your phone) when you want a permanent crew with a bank, perks and persistence.

Being in a group mainly buys you three things: your squadmates show up as friendly (green names/hexes), their **group name floats under their nametag** when you're close, and you share **tactical pings** and **restrain tracking** with them (details in "Why bother grouping" below).

### Where to find it

- **Your phone → "Group" app.** Open your phone (default **Y**), tap the **Group** icon ("Join or create a temporary group").
- There is **no NPC, shop, scroll-wheel option or map marker** for this. It's phone-only.
- **Civilians only.** The Group app only appears for civs. Cops and Trauma Team don't get it (they run off their own faction systems).
- The window is titled **"Group Management."** If you're **not** in a group it opens the **browser** ("Current Groups") so you can join or create one. If you **are** in a group it opens the **member panel** so you can manage it.

### Requirements & costs

- **Creating a group is FREE.** The create screen literally says "Creating a group is free!" No license, item, talent or rank needed.
- You must be a **civilian**.
- You **cannot create or join a group while in Passive Mode.** You'll get "You cannot create/join a group while in passive mode! You can disable it on your phone (Y)." Turn passive off first.
- **Group name rules:** 3–25 characters. Letters, numbers, underscore and spaces only (`A-z 0-9 _`). The name must be **unique** — if someone already has it you'll get "That group name is already taken!"
- **A password is mandatory when you create.** 1–16 characters, same allowed characters as the name. (You can still leave the group **unlocked** afterward so people can join without typing it — see Lock/Unlock.)

### How to create a group

1. Phone → **Group** → **Create** button.
2. Type your **group name** (3–25 chars) in the top field and a **password** (up to 16 chars) in the second field.
3. Click **Create**. You're instantly made **Founder + Leader** of the new group and the management panel opens.
4. New groups start **locked** (password required) by default. Hit **Unlock** if you want anyone to be able to walk in without the password.

**Cooldown:** you can only create a group once every **30 seconds** ("Please wait a bit before creating another group!").

### How to browse & join a group

1. Phone → **Group** (if you're not already in one) → the **Current Groups** list appears.
2. Each entry reads **`Name [Members: X] - [PASS]`** or **`- [UNLOCKED]`**. `[PASS]` means it's locked and needs the password; `[UNLOCKED]` means open to anyone. The list is sorted alphabetically.
3. Select a group and click **Join**.
   - **Unlocked group:** you join immediately — "You have joined the group: <name>".
   - **Locked group:** a "Group Password Required" box pops up. Type the password and click **OK**. Wrong password gives "Incorrect password." and drops you back to the browser.
4. If there are no groups at all, you'll see "No groups are available. Create one instead!" and the Join button is greyed out.

### Managing your group (the member panel)

Open the phone **Group** app while you're in a group to get the **member list** and these buttons. Most are **Leader-only** — non-leaders don't even see them:

- **Leave** — everyone can use this (see below).
- **Set Pass** — change the group password. *Leader only.* Rate-limited to once every **120 seconds** ("You cannot rapidly set passwords!").
- **Kick** — select a member, remove them. *Leader only.* You can't kick yourself.
- **Set Leader** — hand leadership to the selected member. *Leader only.* You drop to a normal member; they become the new leader.
- **Lock** — require the password to join. *Leader or Founder.* 30-second cooldown.
- **Unlock** — let anyone join without the password. *Leader or Founder.* 30-second cooldown.

The panel header shows **`Group: <name> | Pass: <password>`**, and members are tagged **[Leader]** and **[Founder]**.

> **Heads-up:** the password is shown **in plain text** to everyone in the group on that panel. Don't reuse a password you care about, and remember any current member can read it and share it.

**Founder vs Leader:** the **Founder** is whoever created the group (tracked by your Steam ID). The **Leader** is the in-game squad lead. They start as the same person, but **Set Leader** moves leadership without moving the founder tag. Note a Founder who isn't the Leader can still Lock/Unlock, but **Set Pass / Kick / Set Leader require you to actually be the Leader** — otherwise you'll get "You don't have the ability to do that."

### Leaving / getting kicked

- **Leave** removes you from the group. If you're in a **gang**, you're dropped back into your **gang's default group** ("You have left your group. Rejoining your gangs default group."). If you're not in a gang, you get put in a fresh solo group.
- **If the Founder leaves:** ownership automatically passes to another member. If you were the **last** person in the group, the group is **deleted** entirely.
- **If you're kicked:** "You have been kicked out of the group." — same rejoin-your-gang behavior applies.

### Why bother grouping (the actual perks)

- **Friendly identification:** squadmates render with **green names/hexes** (you can toggle "Colored Group Hexes" in phone Settings). Handy for not shooting your own team.
- **Group name under nametags:** when you're within **~25 m** of a squadmate (or looking at them through **binoculars/rangefinder in gunner view**), your group's name shows under their name.
- **Tactical pings:** the **Shift+T** tac ping (verify keybind) is shared with your registered squadmates within **1000 m**. This is the big one — if you're just sitting in the default civilian slot group with strangers, your pings leak to nobody useful; a real group (or gang) is what makes pings go to your actual team.
- **Restrain tracking:** if a squadmate **restrains or blindfolds** someone, **all group members** get an orange map marker reading **"Last Known Location of <name>"** for **5 minutes**. Great for coordinating a grab or a hostage move.

### Gotchas & fine print

- **Nothing persists.** Groups are wiped on server restart and are never saved to the database. Re-form after every restart.
- **A group is not a gang.** No shared bank, no perks, no vehicles, no persistence. If you want those, make a gang.
- **Being in a gang gives you a default group already.** Creating or joining a custom group temporarily pulls you out of your gang's default group; leaving/getting kicked drops you back into it.
- **One group at a time.** Joining a new group replaces whatever group you were in.
- **Names are server-unique and case-sensitive-ish** — pick something distinctive. The system also auto-builds a short **side-chat callsign tag** from the first/middle/last letters of your name (e.g. a name like "Reapers" becomes a tag like "[Rar]"), and if that collides with an existing tag it gets a number appended (verify exact format).
- **Cooldowns to remember:** create **30 s**, lock/unlock **30 s**, change password **120 s**.
- **Character limits:** name **3–25**, password **1–16**, both restricted to letters, numbers, underscore and spaces.

---

## GPS navigation & road routing

LooneyBin has a full in-car GPS. Not a straight line to a marker — it finds an actual **road route** from where you're standing to wherever you point, draws it on your map as a neon-cyan line, and gives you turn-by-turn directions with a banner up top and glowing "breadcrumbs" on the road ahead. If you own the right vehicle upgrade it'll even drive the route for you (Tesla-style autopilot), and if you'd rather not walk back to a parked vehicle you can **Summon Vehicle** it to you instead (see Vehicles).

It routes like a real "fastest route" GPS: it prefers highways and main roads, tolerates smaller roads, and avoids dirt tracks and footpaths.

### Where it lives (there's no phone app for it)

The GPS is opened **from your map**, not from the phone's app grid. Two ways in:

- **ALT + Left-Click** anywhere on the open map → plots a road route straight there.
- **Press G** on the open map → opens the **NAVIGATION** search panel (type a place name).

(Open your map first — M by default — then use one of the above.)

The only thing with its own phone-app icon is **Summon Vehicle** (see the vehicles section). The map GPS itself is map-only.

### What you need

- **A GPS item** in your **assigned items** — this is the standard `ItemGPS` receiver. Buy it for **$10** at any **General Store**, and it's also stocked at gas stations, gun stores and most other virtual shops. It auto-assigns when purchased.
  - The **search panel (G)** requires a GPS in your assigned items — no GPS, and it just tells you "you need a GPS in your assigned items."
  - **Both ALT+Click routing and G-search need a GPS item (`ItemGPS`) in your assigned items** — no GPS, no navigation.
- **No license, no talent, no cost** for the routing itself. Plotting and following a route is free.

### Plotting a route by clicking the map

1. Open your map.
2. **Hold ALT and left-click** the spot you want to go.
3. A cyan route snaps onto the roads, from your current position to that spot, and you get a chat line: *"route plotted — X km by road. ALT+Click near the flag to clear."*
4. To cancel: **ALT+Click within ~200m of the destination flag** (or just ALT+Click the same spot again). You'll see *"route cleared."*

If the spot can't be reached by road, you'll get *"no road route found to there."* — pick a point that's actually on or near a road.

### Searching for a destination

1. Open your map and press **G**.
2. The **NAVIGATION** panel opens. Start typing — the list filters live as you type.
3. What's in the search index (curated, not every dot on the map):
   - **Towns and cities** (Kavala, Pyrgos, Sofia, etc.)
   - **Drug fields and their processors** (weed, heroin/poppy, cocaine/coca, meth/crank, moonshine, mushrooms…)
   - **Federal event sites** (Federal Reserve, Bank, Blackwater-type targets)
4. **Double-click** an entry, or select it and hit **PLOT ROUTE**. The panel closes, your map opens if it wasn't already, and the route draws.
5. **CLEAR ROUTE** wipes the current route; **CLOSE** just closes the panel.

For anything not in that list (a specific house, a random field, a friend's marker) — close the panel and just **ALT+Click** the spot instead.

### Turn-by-turn guidance while you drive

Once a route is active you get live guidance that updates several times a second:

- **A banner at the top-center** of your screen showing the next maneuver — e.g. **`>> TURN RIGHT in 120 m`**, **`< BEAR LEFT`**, **`>>> SHARP RIGHT`**, **`MAKE A U-TURN`**, or **`FOLLOW THE ROAD`** on long straights — plus your remaining distance to the destination (in m, or km once it's over 1 km).
- **In-world neon breadcrumbs and chevrons** floating just over the road ahead of you (roughly the next 240m). These are drawn only on *your* screen — other players don't see them.
- **Auto-reroute:** wander more than ~60m off the route for ~2.5 seconds and it recalculates automatically (*"recalculating…"*). Handy when you miss a turn or take a shortcut.
- **Arrival:** get within ~40m of the destination and it announces *"you have arrived"* and clears itself.
- **Death clears it:** if you die with a route active, it wipes (dead men need no directions).

### Autopilot — let the car drive (Tesla mode)

If your vehicle has the **Autopilot Module** installed, it can drive the plotted route for you, hands-off.

- **Requirements:**
  - The **Autopilot Module** fitted to the vehicle — a **garage/dealership upgrade, $30,000** (ground vehicles only). *(Heads-up: an in-game popup misquotes this as "$300" — the actual charge is $30,000.)*
  - An **active GPS route** plotted (ALT+Click or search).
  - **You in the driver's seat.**
- **To engage:** while in the vehicle, press **TAB** to open the car-radio radial. If the vehicle has the module, three extra wedges appear at the bottom — **MAD MAX**, **NORMAL**, **SLOTH**. Click one to start driving.
- **Driving modes (top speed):**
  - **Sloth** — ~50 km/h, gentle.
  - **Normal** — ~80 km/h.
  - **Mad Max** — up to ~150 km/h, more grip and harder braking.
  - It reads the actual road curvature ahead and slows down for corners in every mode, so hairpins are taken carefully.
- **It disengages automatically** if you touch the controls (any steering/throttle input drops it), if the vehicle takes **25%+ damage**, runs **out of fuel**, gets **stuck/blocked**, reaches the destination, the route ends, or you die.
- Only the **driver** can engage or steer it — the radial will tell a passenger *"Only the driver controls the radio."*

### Don't want to drive at all?

Star a vehicle in the **Garage** app and tap **Summon Vehicle** on the phone — it spawns about a kilometre out and a headless-client driver brings it to you, ground or air. See *Summon Vehicle* under the vehicles section for the full how-to; it isn't a GPS feature (it always comes to wherever you're currently standing, not a plotted destination).

### The Motorist talent path (optional upgrades)

There's a whole civilian talent branch built around driving. Points come from your standard talent tree (Talents app on the phone):

- **Road Warrior (200)** — vehicles you pull come pre-stocked with a Toolkit and 3 First Aid Kits in the trunk. Unlocks the Motorist path.
- **Autopilot Certified (201)** — the **$30,000 Autopilot Module installs FREE** on any vehicle you own, waived at both the garage and the dealership.
- **Chauffeur (202)** — your autopilot gets **+30% cornering grip and +12% speed** in every mode, and stops crawling to a near-halt on the tightest hairpins.
- **Networker (203)** — your GPS **search also reveals live gang turfs, active cartels, and running events** (Gang Fortress, Turf, Purge, captures) — locations other drivers can't search for.
- **Pit Boss (204)** — reinforced frame: **40% less collision and ram damage** to your vehicle.

### Tips & gotchas

- **It's a real road route, not a straight line.** The cyan line follows actual streets — the km figure it quotes is road distance, which can be much longer than the crow-flies distance to a marker.
- **First use has a tiny hitch.** The road map data loads the first time you plot a route in a session (a brief one-time pause), then every route after that is instant.
- **Highways preferred.** Routing weights main roads as fastest and avoids dirt tracks; it will never deliberately send a car down a pedestrian footpath (though a dead-end start can force a short one to escape).
- **Keep a GPS on you** so the search panel works — losing it (e.g. blindfolded/restrained situations temporarily strip it) disables G-search until it's back in your assigned items.
- **Clearing:** ALT+Click near the flag, hit CLEAR ROUTE in the search panel, or just die — any of those wipe the route.
- **Autopilot won't fight you.** Any manual input immediately hands control back — good for dodging, but don't nudge the stick if you want it to keep driving. A car at 25%+ damage also refuses to stay on autopilot until repaired.

---

## Vehicle autopilot & cruise control

Your car can drive itself two different ways, and they are NOT the same feature:

- **Cruise control** — a simple "hold this speed" you can set in almost any vehicle you drive. You still steer.
- **GPS Autopilot ("Tesla mode")** — true hands-off self-driving that follows a plotted GPS route, steers itself around corners, brakes for traffic, and parks at the destination. Ground vehicles only, and it needs a paid upgrade.

There's also **Summon Vehicle** — recall a starred vehicle from your Garage and let a headless-client driver bring it to you (no upgrade or license needed).

### Cruise control (keeps your speed, you keep the wheel)

**What it is:** Locks the throttle at your current speed so your foot can come off the gas on a long highway run. You still steer and you can still crash — it only holds speed.

**Where / how:** There's no shop, item, or scroll menu. While you're **driving**, tap your **"User Action 5"** key. (Same key that toggles auto-run when you're on foot — bind it under **Configure → Controls → Custom Controls → User Action 5** if you haven't; there's no guaranteed default key. (verify exact default))

**Requirements:**
- You must be the **driver** (not a passenger).
- It must be **your vehicle** (a vehicle local to you). If you get "This vehicle is not yours," cruise won't set.
- You can't be **downed** or **restrained**.
- Works in **cars only**. It does **not** work in aircraft or boats (those are non-functional).

**How to use it:**
1. Get up to the speed you want to hold.
2. Tap User Action 5. You'll see "Cruise Control enabled. Brake to disable."
3. **To turn it off, just tap the brake.** That's the only off switch — any brake input drops it instantly.

**Gotchas:**
- It holds the speed you were doing **at the moment you pressed it**, so get to cruising speed first.
- It does not steer, does not avoid anything, and does not slow for corners. It's a highway convenience, not a self-driver.

### GPS Autopilot — the self-driving system

**What it is:** With the Autopilot Module installed, your ground vehicle will drive itself all the way to your plotted GPS destination — steering through corners, slowing for hairpins, braking for cars/people in its lane, and coming to a full stop when it arrives. You sit back and watch. Touch any driving control and it instantly hands the wheel back to you.

Getting it working is three steps: **buy the module → plot a GPS route → engage it from the TAB radial.**

#### Step 1 — Buy the Autopilot Module ($30,000)

- **Where:** At the **car dealer** (when buying/configuring a vehicle) or in your **garage** mod menu. Look for the **Autopilot** option — "No Autopilot" vs "Autopilot Module."
- **Cost: $30,000.** (Heads up: two in-game popups mislabel it as "$300" — the real charge at the register is $30,000. (verify — the price text is a known typo, config value is $30,000))
- **Ground vehicles only.** The option is greyed out for boats and aircraft.
- It's a **permanent per-vehicle upgrade** — buy it once for that car and it stays. A car without the module can't engage autopilot at all (you'll get "this vehicle has no autopilot module").

#### Step 2 — Plot a GPS route

Autopilot follows the cyan GPS route line, so you need one on your map first. Two ways:

- **ALT + Click the map** at where you want to go. It plots a road route from your position and tells you the distance ("route plotted — X km by road"). **ALT+Click near the destination flag again to clear it.** (ALT+Click routing needs a GPS item on you, same as G-search.)
- **Press G with the map open** to open a searchable destination list (towns, drug fields + their processors, federal sites). This search **requires an ItemGPS in your assigned items** — no GPS, no search.

Notes:
- The destination has to be a real drive — if it's basically right where you're standing, autopilot will tell you to "walk it."
- If no road route can be found, you'll get "no road route found."

#### Step 3 — Engage it (TAB radial)

1. Get in the **driver's seat** of a ground vehicle that has the module.
2. Press **TAB** to open the in-car radial (the same wheel you use for the car radio). With the module installed, three extra wedges appear at the bottom: **MAD MAX**, **NORMAL**, and **SLOTH**.
3. **Left-click** the mode you want. Autopilot engages: "engaged — [MODE] mode. Touch the controls to take over."

The car now drives itself to the flag. You can **switch modes on the fly** by opening TAB again and picking a different wedge.

#### Driving modes (speeds)

| Mode | Top speed | Style |
|------|-----------|-------|
| **Sloth** | ~50 km/h | Slow and safe |
| **Normal** | ~80 km/h | Balanced default |
| **Mad Max** | up to **150 km/h** (never below ~60) | Fast — more grip and harder braking, top end scales with your car's real max speed |

In every mode it reads each upcoming corner's radius and slows down **before** the bend so it can physically make the turn — hairpins are always taken slowly. Mad Max just carries more speed and brakes harder everywhere else.

#### Requirements & when autopilot refuses to engage

You'll get a cyan "AUTOPILOT" message and it won't start if:
- The vehicle **has no Autopilot Module.**
- You haven't **set a GPS destination** yet, or the destination is too close.
- You're **not on (or right beside) a public road** — you can't use it to teleport off-road across rivers or out of a firefight.
- You're **more than ~35 m from the plotted route** — drive closer or re-plot.
- The vehicle is **damaged** — any part into the yellow (**25%+ damage**) or hull damage blocks it. Repair first.

#### When autopilot disengages mid-drive

It cuts out (and tells you why) when:
- **You touch any driving control** — throttle, brake, or steering. This is the intended "take over instantly" behavior.
- **You arrive** — it brakes to a full stop at the flag, then disengages.
- The vehicle takes **damage (25%+)**, **runs out of fuel**, gets **blocked/stuck** ("blocked — take the wheel"), or can no longer move.
- **You die.**

#### Gotchas & tips

- **It can still crash.** It plans corners with a safety margin (especially in Mad Max), but a bad line into a tight hairpin can put you into a wall. If you're hauling something valuable, use Normal or Sloth.
- **It stays in its lane and stops for traffic** — a parked truck or a player in front will make it slow and stop rather than shove through. If it's stuck behind something, it'll eventually hand you the wheel.
- **Damage is a hard gate** at both ends: you can't engage a beat-up car, and taking a 25% hit mid-trip kicks you back to manual.
- It runs on **your** client as the driver, so it only self-drives the vehicle you're actually sitting in and driving.
- Bystanders see it drive smoothly — no teleport stutter — so it's fine to autopilot past other players.

### Don't own a car with the module?

Star a vehicle in the **Garage** app and tap **Summon Vehicle** — it comes to you instead. Full details in *Summon Vehicle* under the vehicles section.

### Motorist talents that upgrade all of this

There's a whole civilian talent path built around driving. Spend talent points (phone → Talents) to unlock:

- **Road Warrior (200)** — unlocks the Motorist path. Pulled vehicles come stocked with a Toolkit + 3 First Aid Kits in the trunk.
- **Autopilot Certified (201)** — the **$30,000 Autopilot Module installs FREE** on any vehicle you own, at both the garage and the dealer. (Pays for itself in a single car.)
- **Chauffeur (202)** — your autopilot "drives like it stole it": **+30% cornering grip and +12% top speed** in every mode (Mad Max ceiling rises to roughly **158 km/h**), and it stops crawling to a near-halt on the tightest hairpins.
- **Networker (203)** — your GPS destination **search** also reveals live **gang turfs, active cartels, and running events** (Gang Fortress, Turf, Purge) as searchable destinations.
- **Pit Boss (204)** — reinforced frame: **collision/ram damage reduced by 40%.** Not autopilot-specific, but it softens the occasional autopilot fender-bender. (verify — general vehicle collision reduction, applies whether you're driving or on autopilot)

If you plan to lean on self-driving, **Autopilot Certified (201)** and **Chauffeur (202)** are the two to grab.

## Summon Vehicle (recall your starred vehicle) — Player Wiki

Recall one of your own vehicles without walking back to it. Star a vehicle in the **Garage** app, tap **SUMMON VEHICLE**, and it spawns about a kilometre out and comes to you on its own — driven the rest of the way by a headless-client driver, not by you.

### Where to find it

- **Garage app** (phone) — every vehicle row has a **★** toggle. Tap it to make that vehicle your **recall vehicle**. Only one at a time; tapping a different row moves the star.
- **SUMMON VEHICLE** tile on the phone launcher — shows your recall vehicle's name and kind, its current state, and one **SUMMON** button.

### Requirements

- **A vehicle starred** in the Garage app. Any kind can be starred — car, truck, helicopter, VTOL — except planes and boats, which are refused at star time ("can't be summoned").
- **A headless-client driver online.** No driver, no summon: *"no driver available right now."*
- **On foot, not seated** in any vehicle.
- **Not on cooldown**, and not already mid-summon.

### How it works

1. Star a vehicle in the **Garage** app.
2. Open **SUMMON VEHICLE** and tap **SUMMON**.
3. It spawns roughly **1 km** away and heads for you:
   - **Ground vehicles** drive the road route in, same as any autopilot delivery.
   - **Helicopters and VTOLs** fly themselves on the engine's own AI with an invisible pilot at the stick — a helicopter **lands beside you**; a VTOL **flies in, then descends straight down** onto a clear spot near you.
4. The tile tracks it live: **GARAGED → EN ROUTE (distance, ETA) → ARRIVED**, or a **FAILED** reason if something went wrong.
5. On arrival it's unlocked and yours — if it was already out in the world, fuel, damage and trunk contents carry over rather than resetting.

### Cost and cooldown

- **Free.** No fare, no delivery charge.
- **120-second cooldown** after a completed summon before you can call it again.

### When it refuses

- **No headless-client driver online.**
- **Nothing starred** — star a vehicle in the Garage app first.
- **You're seated** in a vehicle — get out first.
- **Still on cooldown.**
- **The recall vehicle is already out in the world and someone's in it.**
- **The recall vehicle is wrecked.**

### Gotchas

- Only **one** recall vehicle at a time, and only **one** summon in flight at a time.
- If it's already out somewhere, summoning **picks it up and redelivers it** — it doesn't duplicate the vehicle.
- A failed summon (stuck, blocked, wrecked, timed out) leaves the vehicle wherever it stopped, unlocked, with its grid location — and does **not** burn the cooldown.
- It always comes to **wherever you're currently standing**, not a plotted GPS destination — there's no route to set beforehand.

---

## In-vehicle car radio stations & synced music

Every drivable vehicle on LooneyBin has a working car stereo. Turn it on and the whole cabin hears music; people standing near your car hear it too, coming out of the vehicle. It works like real radio: each station is a nonstop broadcast run by the server, so everyone tuned to the same station is hearing the exact same song at the exact same moment. Jump into a taxi mid-song and you pick up right where everyone else is, not from the top.

### What it is

- **5 stations**, each its own genre. What you'll actually hear right now:
  - **Pulse** — electronic / EDM dance (Daft Punk, Deadmau5, Swedish House Mafia, a Skrillex remix, and more).
  - **Classico** — classical (Vivaldi, Beethoven, Mozart, Bach, Pachelbel, Wagner, etc.).
  - **Anarchy** — rock / metal (AC/DC, Ozzy Osbourne, KISS, Lynyrd Skynyrd, Motley Crue, Rush, and friends).
  - **Origin** and **KHR** — these two may be **silent** unless the current station lineup has been loaded onto them. If you tune to Origin or KHR and hear nothing, that's why — switch to Pulse, Classico, or Anarchy. (verify — station lineups can be updated from the backend without a server restart, so Origin/KHR may be playing by the time you read this)
- **Occupants** (anyone sitting in the vehicle) hear the music in full, in stereo, synced to the live broadcast position.
- **Bystanders** (people on foot near the car) hear it coming out of the vehicle, positional and getting fainter with distance — it fades out by about **20 metres** away.

### Where

There's no shop, NPC, phone app, or map marker for this — the radio lives on the vehicle itself and is worked entirely with keys while you're in the driver's seat. No purchase, no install, nothing to unlock.

### Requirements

- **None.** No license, no item, no cash cost, no talent, no rank. Every player has it on every vehicle they drive, for free.
- You must be the **driver** (or pilot) to turn the radio on/off or change the station. Passengers can listen but can't touch the dial.

### How to use it — controls

You need to be **sitting in a vehicle** for any of these to do anything.

- **Q** — toggle the radio **on / off** (ground vehicles and boats).
- **R** — jump to the **next station** (only while the radio is already on).
- **TAB** — open the **radial station picker**, a half-circle wheel of station "wedges" that fans across the top of your screen, each in its own color with its logo. Move your mouse over a wedge to highlight it, read the station name in the panel to the right, and **left-click** to tune to it. There's a separate **OFF** wedge (power icon) sitting by itself at the bottom to kill the radio. Press **TAB** or **ESC** to close the wheel.

### Step-by-step

1. Get into a vehicle and take the **driver's** seat.
2. Press **Q**. You'll see a "Radio ON" message and a **Now Playing** card slides in at the lower-left with the station logo and current song title.
3. If you don't like what's on (or it's silent), press **R** to cycle to the next station, or press **TAB** and click the station you want on the wheel.
4. Anyone riding with you hears it instantly, synced to the same spot in the song you're hearing. People walking past your parked car hear it out to ~20 m.
5. Press **Q** again (or click **OFF** on the TAB wheel) to shut it off.

### In aircraft

Helicopters and planes have a radio too, but **Q and R are flight controls there** (collective and reload), so they won't work the stereo. In aircraft the **TAB wheel is the only way** to turn the radio on, switch stations, or turn it off. Note: people on the ground **don't** hear an aircraft's radio — only the people inside the aircraft do. (Ground vehicles, boats, and tanks broadcast to bystanders; aircraft don't.)

### The "Now Playing" card

Whenever your station changes song (or you switch stations), a card slides in from the lower-left edge showing the station's colored logo and the track title/artist. It sits for about **6 seconds**, then slides back out. When the radio is off it briefly shows **"RADIO OFF — PRESS Q IN A VEHICLE."** Only occupants get this card; bystanders just hear the audio.

### Numbers at a glance

- Stations: **5** (Origin, KHR, Pulse, Classico, Anarchy).
- Bystander hearing range: **~20 metres**, fading with distance.
- Now Playing card: on screen ~**6 seconds**.
- Cost to use: **$0** — completely free.
- Songs currently loaded: Pulse, Classico, and Anarchy each carry **10 tracks**; Origin and KHR may be empty (see above).

### Gotchas

- **Turn it on and hear silence?** The radio defaults to the first station (Origin) when you first switch it on, and Origin may have no music loaded. Press **R** a couple of times or use the **TAB** wheel to land on Pulse, Classico, or Anarchy.
- **Hear nothing even on a good station?** The in-car music plays through your game's **Music volume slider** (Audio options). If that slider is at zero, occupants hear nothing — bump it up. (Bystander audio is separate and isn't affected by the Music slider.)
- **You can't skip a song.** Because each station is one shared live broadcast, there's no "next track" — **R only changes the whole station**, it doesn't skip within a station. You'll hear whatever the station is playing right now, same as everyone else tuned in.
- **The radio keeps playing after you get out.** Turning it on latches it to the vehicle. It keeps broadcasting even if the driver leaves the seat or logs off — it only stops when a driver toggles it off, or the car is stored in a garage / destroyed.
- **Passengers can't change it.** Only the driver/pilot controls the station. If you're not driving and try, you'll get "Only the driver controls the radio."
- **Occasional "Moving to Malden" announcement.** Every station drops a LooneyBin announcement clip roughly **every 3rd track** — that's intentional, not a glitch.
- **A wheel with extra wedges?** On some vehicles the TAB wheel may show extra driving-mode wedges (e.g. MAD MAX / NORMAL / SLOTH) below the stations — that's the separate vehicle **autopilot** feature riding on the same wheel, only shown when a vehicle has autopilot enabled and you're its driver. It has nothing to do with the music.
- **Everyone's in sync.** If a friend says they're hearing a different part of the song than you, double-check you're both actually tuned to the **same station** — same station always means same song at the same moment.

---

## Boombox (deployable music speaker)

### What it is
The Boom Box is a virtual item you buy, carry in your inventory, and then drop on the ground as a physical speaker (it looks like a small black battery pack). Once it's down you can play looping music from it that other players nearby can hear, and anyone standing close can bust out a dance. It's pure fun/RP flavor — no money, no cartel, no crime. The one catch: it self-destructs after 10 minutes, so it's a party prop, not permanent furniture.

### Where to get it
Buy the **Boom Box** item from a general store:
- **Town Market** — the main "[Map] Market" shop found in town centers.
- **Hunting Item Shop.**

It's a normal virtual-shop purchase (open the shop, find "Boom Box" in the buy list, buy it). No NPC quest, no phone app, no map marker needed.

### Requirements
- **Item:** You just need to own a Boom Box (buy one). No license, no talent, no rank, no prestige required. Any civilian can use it.
- **Inventory weight:** 3 (make sure you have the space).
- **To place one you must:**
  - Be **on foot** (not sitting in a vehicle).
  - Have at least one Boom Box in your inventory.
  - **Not already have a Boom Box out** — you can only have **one active at a time**.
  - Not currently be placing another object.
  - **Not be within 20m of another Boom Box** (yours or anyone else's) — you can't cram them together.

### Cost
- **Buy price: about $1,000** each (this is the base price; the exact number can tick up a little with the Governor's sales tax, and it's 15% cheaper if you buy inside your own gang's Gang Fortress).
- **Placing one consumes the item** — it's used up when you set it down. Deleting it or letting it expire gives you **no refund**, so you need a fresh Boom Box every time you want to deploy one.
- It does **not appear to be sellable back to any shop** — treat it as a one-use consumable.

### Step-by-step: how to use it
1. **Buy a Boom Box** ($1,000) from a Town Market or the Hunting Item Shop.
2. Stand where you want it, **on foot**. Scroll-wheel and pick the orange **"Place Boom Box"** option.
3. You go into placement mode — the boombox floats in front of you. Fine-tune it with the scroll-wheel options: **Rotate Object (10 deg)**, **Raise/Lower Object**, then choose **"Place Object"** to set it down. (Choosing **"Stop Placing Object"** cancels with no cost — the item is only used up when you actually place it.)
4. On placement you'll see: *"Your boom box is now ready for music to be played! Your boombox will automatically delete in 10 minutes!"* — the countdown starts now.
5. **Aim your crosshair at the boombox** and scroll. As the owner you'll get:
   - **Start Music** (orange)
   - **Stop Music** (red) — only shows while a song is playing
   - **Delete Boom Box** (red)
6. Pick **Start Music**. It asks you Yes/No for each track in order — say **No** to skip to the next, **Yes** to play that one. Available tracks:
   - NCS | Alan Walker – Fade
   - Deaf Kev – Invincible
   - Different Heaven – My Heart
   - Shipwrek and Zookeepers – Ark
   - Daniel Levi and Cartoon – On and On
7. The track **loops automatically** and you get *"Your boom box has started playing [song]!"* Use **Start Music** again to switch songs, or **Stop Music** (Yes/No confirm) to kill the audio.
8. Anyone standing **within 13m** of a boombox gets a **"Start Dancing"** scroll option (and **"Stop Dancing"** to stop) — this plays a random dance animation. It's open to everyone nearby, not just the owner.
9. When you're done, **Delete Boom Box** (Yes/No confirm) removes it early. Otherwise it disappears on its own at the 10-minute mark.

### Exact numbers & timers
- **Buy price:** ~$1,000 (base). **Weight:** 3.
- **Lifespan:** exactly **10 minutes (600 seconds)** from placement, then it auto-deletes.
- **Limit:** **one boombox out per player** at a time.
- **No-overlap radius:** can't place within **20m** of any existing boombox.
- **Dance range:** stand within **13m** to get the dance option; a dance lasts up to **10 minutes** or until you stop it.
- **How far the music carries:** the sound itself is audible to roughly **~60m** around the boombox (verify exact distance). Players within about **350m** get the loop synced to them (including people who walk up or relog while it's playing), but you generally need to be fairly close to actually hear it.

### Gotchas
- **It's a consumable.** Placing it uses up the item; expiry and manual deletion refund nothing. Want it out again? Buy another.
- **Only you control your music.** Start/Stop/Delete are owner-only (matched to your Steam ID) — other players can't touch your song. But **anyone nearby can dance** to it.
- **One at a time.** You can't place a second boombox until your current one is deleted or expires.
- **It vanishes after 10 minutes — automatically.** Don't rely on it sticking around; it's not house furniture.
- **Placement blockers:** you can't place it while in a vehicle, while already placing something else, or within 20m of another boombox.
- **Get close to hear it.** The audio only reaches a short range (~60m), so a boombox on the far side of a town square won't be heard across the whole area.
- **Can't be shot/destroyed.** While placed, the boombox is invulnerable to gunfire — it only goes away when you delete it or it times out.
- **Auto re-sync:** if you relog or the music seems to cut out, being within ~350m of a playing boombox restarts the loop for you automatically.
- **Skip-through menu:** the Start Music picker is a chain of Yes/No prompts, one per song. Say "No" to all of them and nothing plays — just re-open Start Music and pick one.

---

## Time Trials / street racing

Race a spawned-in car or kart around a fixed checkpoint course, against the clock, for a cash payout. It's a solo civilian activity — you're beating the course (and your own times), not other players.

### What it is

You walk up to the Time Trials NPC, pick a course, and a race vehicle spawns just for you. You hop in, line up at the start, wait out a 3-2-1 countdown, then blast through a chain of numbered checkpoints in order until you cross the finish. Finish the course and you get paid a flat amount. Your time is shown on the finish screen for bragging rights, but the payout is the same whether you were fast or slow — see Payouts below.

There are two courses right now:
- **Alpha Course** — a proper road lap in a **Hatchback Sport**, 14 checkpoints then a finish line.
- **Go-Kart Course** — a tight little **Go-Kart** circuit (3 quick laps of the same small track), 20 checkpoints then a finish.

### Where

- On the map, look for the **green car icon labelled "Go-Karts / Time Trials."** It sits near the middle of Altis, a little northwest of map centre (verify exact town name).
- Walk up to the **Time Trials NPC** standing at the karts. You'll get two scroll-wheel options:
  - **"Start AlphaCourse time trial"**
  - **"Start Go-Kart time trial"**
- (The same spot also has a separate kart/car **rental** NPC right next to it — that's a different guy. For time trials use the one whose scroll options say "Start ... time trial.")

### Requirements

- **You must be a civilian.** Cops, Trauma Team/medics, etc. get "Time trials are currently only available for civilians" and can't start.
- **You must be on foot** to see and use the start option (get out of any vehicle first).
- **No license, no item, no talent, no rank needed**, and **it's free to attempt** — no entry fee.
- You can only have **one time trial active at a time.**
- The spawn point has to be **clear** — if another vehicle is parked on it you'll get "There is currently already a vehicle on the spawnpoint." Wait for it to clear or pull it away.

### Step-by-step: how to run a trial

1. Get out of your vehicle and scroll on the NPC. Pick **Start AlphaCourse** or **Start Go-Kart**.
2. Your **race vehicle spawns** at a marked point and a waypoint says **"Get in the time trial vehicle."** Run over and get in **quickly** — don't wander off (see gotchas).
3. Once you're in, a waypoint reads **"Move to the start and await the countdown."** Drive to the start line.
4. **Stop dead on the start line and hold still.** When you're stopped in the start zone a **3 - 2 - 1** countdown ticks down. If you roll or creep, the countdown **resets to 4** and starts over, so keep the car completely stationary.
5. On **"Go!"** the timer starts. Hit every checkpoint **in order** — each one pops **"Checkpoint N passed."** A bright primary waypoint marks your next checkpoint and a dimmer one shows the one after it.
6. Cross the **finish** checkpoint to complete the run. You'll see **"Time trial complete in Xs — you earned $Y!"** and get paid.
7. Want to bail mid-run? While a trial is active you get a red **"Stop time trial"** self scroll option that cancels it (counts as a fail, no payout).

### Payouts (exact)

Payout is a **flat amount per course**, paid on finish:

- **Alpha Course (Hatchback):** **$20,000**
- **Go-Kart Course:** **$22,500**

Your finish **time does not change the payout** — a clean slow lap pays exactly the same as a record lap. There's effectively no time limit either: the run only auto-fails if you somehow take longer than ~9,999 seconds (about 2.75 hours), so treat time as pride only.

### Quests tied to racing

Completing any time trial ticks these civilian quests:
- **"Speed Racer"** — complete **any** time trial once → **$7,500** (one-time restart quest).
- **"Career Racer"** — complete any time trial **125 times** → **$45,000** (lifetime quest).

So beyond the per-run cash, grinding trials chips away at both of those.

### Gotchas — things that FAIL your run

Failing ends the trial with a red "You have failed the time trial…" message and **no payout**. You fail if you:

- **Get out of the race vehicle** at any point after entering it.
- **Wander too far from the start** before you get going — more than **150m** from the spawned car before you get in, or more than **200m** from the start line during the countdown phase.
- **Open your inventory, map, or gear menu** during the run — the game reads this as "attempting to cheat the course" and fails you. Keep those closed while racing.
- **Get towed, sling-loaded, or sit in another vehicle's cargo** — also flagged as cheating.
- **Leave the track** — get more than **2,000m** away from your current checkpoint and it's "leaving the tracks."
- Hit **"Stop time trial"** yourself.

Other things worth knowing:

- **Nitro is disabled during time trials** ("Nitro is not allowed during time trials") — you race on the car's stock power.
- The race car is **keyed to you only.** Other players trying to get in are told "This is not your time trial vehicle! Get your own!"
- **After you finish or fail, that vehicle is dead to you** — it goes into a "destructive state" and you can't get back in. Start a new trial for a fresh car.
- The race vehicle **cleans itself up** when your run ends, if you leave/die, or after about **30 minutes** — don't count on keeping it.
- **Checkpoint tolerances differ by course:** the Alpha (Hatchback) course is forgiving (roughly a 12m radius per checkpoint, 3m to sit on the start line); the **Go-Kart course is tight** (about a 4m checkpoint radius and you have to stop within ~1m of the start line to trigger the countdown). Expect to be more precise in the kart.

### Multiplayer / head-to-head street racing

There's currently **no player-vs-player or organized multiplayer race** system — the "join race / start race" framework exists in the code but is **switched off/non-functional**. For now "street racing" here means **solo time trials against the clock** at the karts. If you want to race friends, everyone can run the same course separately and compare finish times, but the game won't line you up on a shared grid or track it as a group race.

---

## Duel Arena (1v1 duels)

A hidden, consensual player-vs-player betting arena. Two civilians put up a shared cash pot, get teleported into a sealed walled compound with identical gear, and fight a best-of-3. Winner walks away with 90% of the pot; the loser eats their stake. It's completely voluntary — nobody can force you in, and it has no effect on your wanted level, gang, or anything else. There is **no map marker** for it; you have to know where to go.

### What it is

- A private 1v1 gun duel for money. You and your opponent each put up **half** the total wager, so the pot is split evenly.
- Everyone fights with the **exact same fixed loadout** (an MX Black rifle kit) — no custom gear, no pay-to-win. Pure aim and positioning decide it.
- It's a **best of 3**: first player to win **2 rounds** takes the pot.
- Only **one duel can run on the whole server at a time**, and only **one invite can be sent every 70 seconds server-wide**. If someone else is dueling or just invited, you wait your turn.

### Where

- **The Duel Arena NPC** — a civilian standing at roughly grid **230 171**, in the eastern part of Altis, right outside a walled military compound at about `[22988, 17106]`. (Exact town name — verify.) There is no marker, so bring a friend who knows the spot or memorize the coordinates.
- Scroll on that NPC gives you two options: **"Invite someone to duel"** and **"Accept a duel."**
- The **"Accept a duel"** option is *also* available on every **Courthouse NPC** around the map (Kavala, Pyrgos, Athira, Sofia, etc.). The invite message even tells the person you invited to go to a courthouse. So the inviter uses the arena NPC, but the invitee can accept from any courthouse *or* the arena NPC.

### Requirements

- You must be a **civilian**. Cops/MaxSec cannot invite, accept, or fight (verify — West side is blocked throughout).
- **Money in your BANK**, not cash on hand. You need at least **half the total wager** sitting in your bank account to send an invite *and* to accept one.
- The **total wager must be between $50,000 and $999,999** (you must enter more than $49,999 and less than $1,000,000).
- The person you invite must be a **civilian, alive, not under arrest, and not restrained/cuffed**. Anyone who's downed, jailed, or tied up won't show up as a valid target.

### How to challenge someone (inviter)

1. Go to the Duel Arena NPC and scroll **"Invite someone to duel."** The **"LoonyBin duel arena"** menu opens.
2. In the **"Duel bet price"** box, type the **total pot** you want to fight over — some number from **$50,000 to $999,999**. (This is the *whole* pot; you'll personally pay half of it.)
3. Pick your opponent from the player list on the left, then click **"Invite."**
4. Confirm the "Are you sure you want to invite [name] for a duel with a total of $[amount]?" prompt.
5. Your invite is sent and you wait. You have about **65 seconds** for them to accept. Nothing is deducted from your account yet.
6. If they accept, **half the wager is pulled from your bank** and you're teleported into the arena. If they don't accept in time, the invite expires and you're charged nothing.

### How to accept a challenge (invitee)

1. You'll get a message: "You have been invited for a duel by [name] for a total of $[amount]. You can accept this duel by going to the courthouse and interacting with the NPC (you have one minute)."
2. Head to any **Courthouse NPC** (or back to the Duel Arena NPC) and scroll **"Accept a duel."**
3. Confirm the "Are you sure you want to fight [name]..." prompt.
4. **Half the wager is pulled from your bank** immediately and you're teleported into the arena.
5. You have about **1 minute** from the invite before it expires.

### The fight

- Both fighters spawn in **opposite corners** of the walled compound with the **same loadout**: an **MX Black rifle** with Arco optic + flashlight, roughly **16 magazines** total, a wetsuit + tactical vest, aviator sunglasses, and integrated night-vision (so night duels are fine). **No sidearm, no launcher, no backpack.**
- A **5-second countdown** (5…4…3…2…1) plays, then **"Fight!"** You can't move or shoot until it hits "Fight!"
- Kill your opponent to win the round. Between rounds you'll see "Next round is commencing, the current score is X – Y," everyone resets and re-spawns in the corners, and another 5-second countdown runs.
- **First to 2 round wins takes the pot.** The whole thing plays out inside the compound — don't leave.

### Payouts, prices & timers (exact numbers)

- **Your stake:** half the total wager, taken from your **bank** the moment the duel starts. Example: $100,000 wager → **$50,000 out of each player's bank.**
- **Winner's prize:** the full wager **minus a 10% "judges' tax"** = **wager × 0.90.** On a $100,000 pot the winner receives **$90,000**.
  - Net result on a $100k duel: **winner +$40,000**, **loser –$50,000**, $10,000 vanishes as tax.
- **Refunds:** if the duel fails, aborts, or no winner can be decided, **each player gets their half back** (net zero for both).
- **Wager range:** **$50,000 – $999,999** total pot.
- **Invite cooldown:** one invite every **70 seconds** across the entire server.
- **Invite lifetime:** about **60 seconds** to accept before it expires (~65s hard cutoff).
- **Round countdown:** **5 seconds** before each round.
- **Arena occupancy:** **one duel at a time**, server-wide.

### Gotchas & warnings

- **It's a shared, server-wide resource.** Only one duel runs at once and only one invite goes out every 70 seconds. Popular nights, expect to wait ("The arena is already in use, wait your turn!").
- **Money comes from your BANK, not your pocket.** If your half of the wager isn't in your bank account, the invite/accept is refused.
- **Your Y-inventory is NOT carried into the duel or restored the same way.** You fight with only the fixed event loadout — messages warn twice that "Y inventory will not be restored." Your **weapons/clothing/skins and your position ARE saved and returned** to you when the duel ends, and you're teleported back exactly where you were.
- **Interference voids the duel.** If a third party (not your opponent) kills a fighter, or if a fighter disconnects/vanishes, the arena aborts with no winner and **both players are refunded their half** — so you can lose a won fight to an outsider and get nothing but your stake back. The arena is walled off to prevent this, but be aware.
- **You can't cancel mid-fight.** Once both accept, the stakes are locked in escrow until someone reaches 2 round wins (or it aborts and refunds).
- **No wanted-level or gang consequences** — kills here don't make you wanted and don't count toward war rating/bounties. It's an isolated sandbox.
- **Gear doesn't matter, aim does.** Both sides get the identical MX kit, so don't bother "gearing up" first. Bank a big enough balance and bring your shooting.
- **It's undocumented on purpose** — no marker, not in any menu. If you don't already know the NPC's location, you won't stumble into it.

---

## Crafting / Factory (recipe-based weapon & vehicle crafting)

The Factory system lets you turn raw and processed materials (iron, oil, glass, diamonds, drugs, uranium, gold) plus some cash into finished **vehicles, aircraft, firearms, magazines, optics, and vests**. There are three separate factories, each with its own recipe list. You can either **Keep** what you produce (it goes to you / your garage) or, for most weapons, **Sell** it instantly for cash.

> Note: This covers the general weapon & vehicle factories only. Weed/drug paraphernalia and alcohol "purification factory" recipes are documented in their own sections.

### Where to craft (the three factory NPCs)

Walk up to the right factory worker and use the scroll-wheel action **"Access Factory Systems"** (or **"Black Market Production"** at the black market). You must be **civilian** — cops and TT can't use factories.

| Factory | What it makes | Scroll action | Approx. map location |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Vehicle Production** | Civ cars, vans, trucks, light helis | "Access Factory Systems" | West-central Altis, around grid **[6221, 16265]** (verify town) |
| **Firearm Production** | Legal-ish pistols/SMGs, mags, optics | "Access Factory Systems" | Far NE Altis near Sofia, around **[25400, 20333]** |
| **Black Market Production** | Illegal rifles, armored/armed vehicles, attack helis, explosives, vests | "Black Market Production" | Two spots: near Athira **[5391, 17903]** and central-east **[20745, 15677]** |

The black-market NPC also offers the **Black Market** virtual shop and a **"Break Down Gold Bar"** action (needs a gold bar on you) at the same spot.

### Requirements at a glance

- **Materials + cash:** You must have every listed ingredient in your **virtual inventory**, plus any cash cost shown. The ingredient panel colors items **green** if you have enough and **red** if you're short.
- **Rebel License** ($12,500): required to **Keep** any **7.62mm rifle** (Rahim, Mk18 EBR, Mk-I EMR, Mk14, AKM, AK-12, SPAR-17). You do **not** need it to craft-and-Sell them.
- **Bounty Hunter License** ($12,500): required to **Keep** the **Hunter (B_MRAP_01)**, and the **MX / MX (khaki)** rifles (verify — MX isn't in the standard black-market list). Again, only for Keep, not Sell.
- **Arms Cartel control (gang):** Five items only **appear in the list** if your gang currently holds the Arms Cartel — the **Armed Offroad, Armed Prowler, SPMG, RPG-7, and Suicide Vest**.
- **Space / garage room:** Weapons/mags/vests need free inventory space. Vehicles need an open, unblocked spawn point nearby and a garage that isn't full (**max 45 vehicles** per garage).
- No talent or gang-rank requirement to open the menu itself.

### How to craft (step by step)

1. Use the scroll action at the factory NPC to open the **Factory Systems** window.
2. Pick an item from the left-hand recipe list. The right side shows its **name, description/stats, sell price**, and the **materials** needed (green = have it, red = missing).
3. For **vehicles**, use the drop-down under the list to pick a **color/skin** (only skins you have access to appear).
4. Click one of the bottom buttons:
   - **Keep** — consumes the materials and gives you the item (weapon in hand/inventory, or vehicle spawned outside with keys).
   - **Sell** — consumes the materials and pays you cash instead (weapons/optics/vests only).
   - **Close** — cancel.
5. Confirm any pop-ups (DLC warning, "you already have a weapon equipped — replace it?", etc.).
6. Done. Weapons appear instantly; vehicles spawn locked, **full fuel**, parked at the nearest factory spawn point, and are added to your garage.

### Keep vs Sell

- **Keep** = you get the actual item. Vehicles go to your **garage** (persistent) with keys.
- **Sell** = instant payout at **65% of the item's base factory price** (shown in green as "Sell Price" in the panel). This is a great way to **launder drugs into cash** — e.g. a Katiba costs 2 Iron Ore + 8 Opium and sells for ~$3,250.
- **You cannot Sell** vehicles, aircraft, or magazines — the Sell button greys out for those. Only firearms, optics, and vests are sellable.
- **Where the money lands:**
  - **Firearm-factory guns/optics & vests** → deposited to your **bank**, full 65%.
  - **Black-market rifles** (Katiba, DMR/EMR/Mk14 series, AKM, AK-12, SPAR-17, SPMG, Mk200) → paid as **cash in hand**, and the **Arms Cartel skims 15%** unless your gang holds the cartel.

### Discounts

- **Panagia turf (25% off):** If your **gang currently holds the Panagia turf**, all recipe amounts — including the cash cost — drop to **75%** (rounded up). The saving shows live in the ingredient panel.
- **Lifetime quest discounts (stack on top of turf):**
  - **"Black Market (Air)" (Quest 490)** — craft 100 aircraft (excludes M-900 & MH-9): **-10% on aircraft cash cost**.
  - **"Black Market (Armor)" (Quest 491)** — craft 150 Ifrits/Hunters: **-10% on all materials for Ifrit & Hunter**.
  - **"Black Market (7.62)" (Quest 492)** — craft 200 7.62mm rifles: **-10% on all materials for any crafted firearm** (plus 5 free MK-1s).

### Arms Cartel & Infamy warnings

- **Arms Cartel gating:** The **Armed Offroad, Armed Prowler, SPMG, RPG-7, and Suicide Vest** won't even show up unless your gang owns the Arms Cartel. Selling black-market rifles while your gang *doesn't* own it costs you **15%** off the top.
- **Infamy (heat) from black-market crafting — Keep only:**
  - **Keeping a black-market vehicle** (Ifrit, Armed Offroad, unarmed attack Orcas, etc.) → about **+10 infamy**. The plain black unarmed **Offroad** adds none.
  - **Keeping a black-market rifle** → about **+5 infamy**. The three drug-made rifle mags (7.62x39, 7.62x51, 6.5 caseless green) add none.
  - **Selling** items adds **no** infamy — only Keeping does.

### Vehicle Production Factory — recipes

Cash costs are the cash portion of the recipe (deducted from money on hand). Vehicles can only be **Kept**, not Sold.

| Vehicle | Cash | Materials |
|---|---|---|
| Hatchback | — | 5 Iron Ingot, 4 Rubber, 2 Processed Oil |
| SUV | — | 5 Iron Ingot, 4 Rubber, 2 Processed Oil |
| Hatchback Sport | — | 8 Iron Ingot, 4 Rubber, 4 Processed Oil |
| Van (Transport) | — | 13 Iron Ingot, 4 Rubber, 8 Processed Oil |
| Van (Box) | — | 15 Iron Ingot, 4 Rubber, 8 Processed Oil |
| Zamak Transport | $2,500 | 25 Iron Ingot, 10 Processed Oil, 4 Rubber |
| Zamak Covered | $5,000 | 25 Iron Ingot, 10 Processed Oil, 6 Rubber |
| HEMTT Transport | $7,000 | 30 Iron Ingot, 10 Processed Oil, 6 Rubber |
| HEMTT Box | $15,000 | 30 Iron Ingot, 10 Processed Oil, 8 Rubber |
| M-900 light heli | $25,000 | 6 Rubber, 4 Glass, 10 Processed Oil |
| MH-9 Hummingbird | $5,000 | 6 Rubber, 4 Glass, 20 Diamond Cut, 20 Processed Oil |
| CH-49 Mohawk | $42,000 | 1 Flawless Diamond, 7 Glass, 10 Rubber, 20 Processed Oil |
| Orca, unarmed | $22,000–$25,000 | 1 Flawless Diamond, 5–10 Glass, 20 Diamond Cut, 10 Processed Oil, 10 Rubber |

(The two unarmed Orca variants also appear at the Black Market and count as black-market items for infamy.)

### Firearm Production Factory — recipes

These don't need a rebel license and can be **Sold to bank** at 65%.

| Item | Materials | Sell price (65%) |
|---|---|---|
| ACP-C2 pistol | 6 Iron Ore, 2 Copper Ingot | $1,625 |
| PDW2000 | 10 Iron Ore, 5 Copper Ingot | $2,275 |
| Sting SMG | 8 Iron Ore, 5 Refined Salt | $2,600 |
| Vermin SMG | 10 Iron Ore, 7 Refined Salt | $2,600 |
| .45 ACP mag (9rnd) | 1 Iron Ore | (not sellable) |
| .45 ACP mag (30rnd) | 1 Iron Ore | (not sellable) |
| 9x21 mag (30rnd) | 1 Iron Ore | (not sellable) |
| ACO / Holosight | 1 Refined Salt | $162 |
| ARCO / ERCO / MRCO / HAMR | 1 Copper Ingot, 2 Glass | $536 |

### Black Market Production — recipes

**Rifles & mags** (rebel license needed to *Keep* the 7.62s; all count for infamy except the drug-mags):

| Weapon | Materials | Sell (65%) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|

### Material name glossary

Recipe items map to these virtual-inventory names: Iron Ore, **Iron Ingot** (refined iron), **Copper Ingot** (refined copper), **Refined Salt**, **Glass**, **Rubber**, **Processed Oil**, **Diamond Uncut**, **Diamond Cut**, **Flawless Diamond**, **Gold Nugget**, **Opium** (unprocessed heroin), **Unprocessed Cocaine**, **Crystal Meth**, **Uranium (Weapon Grade)**, Turtle Meat.

### Gotchas

- **Vehicle spawn is blocked if crowded.** A crafted vehicle needs a clear spawn pad within ~100m (no car/boat within 5m, no aircraft within 14m). If it's blocked you get "There is a vehicle currently blocking the spawn point(s)" — move the blocking vehicle and retry.
- **Garage cap (45).** If your garage already holds 45 vehicles you can't craft another one in — you must sell or crash one first.
- **The 7.62 license trick:** No rebel license? You still can't **Keep** a 7.62 rifle, but you **can** craft-and-**Sell** it for cash. Same idea for the bounty-license items.
- **Selling ≠ profit on premium gear.** The Suicide Vest sells for only ~$52,000 but costs $150,000 + gold + uranium — it's meant to be used, not flipped.
- **Discounts stack:** Panagia turf (25% off) applies first, then quest discounts (10%), so a maxed-out player crafting the right item pays noticeably less than the base tables above.
- **Duplicate-equip warning:** Keeping a rifle/pistol/vest while you already have one equipped will prompt to **replace** it — the old one is dropped/lost, so store it first if you want to keep both.
- **Black-market crafting is loud.** Building and keeping illegal guns/vehicles raises your **infamy**, which feeds your notoriety — don't spam-craft attack helis and Ifrits if you're trying to stay low.
